Tool result:
Run systematic **technical** quality checks and generate a comprehensive report. Don't fix issues — document them for other commands to address.

This is a code-level audit, not a design critique. Check what's measurable and verifiable in the implementation.

## Diagnostic Scan

Run comprehensive checks across 5 dimensions. Score each dimension 0-4 using the criteria below.

### 1. Accessibility (A11y)

**Check for**:
- **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA)
- **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps
- **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions
- **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators

**Score 0-4**: 0=Inaccessible (fails WCAG A), 1=Major gaps (few ARIA labels, no keyboard nav), 2=Partial (some a11y effort, significant gaps), 3=Good (WCAG AA mostly met, minor gaps), 4=Excellent (WCAG AA fully met, approaches AAA)

### 2. Performance

**Check for**:
- **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets, missing will-change
- **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization

**Score 0-4**: 0=Severe issues (layout thrash, unoptimized everything), 1=Major problems (no lazy loading, expensive animations), 2=Partial (some optimization, gaps remain), 3=Good (mostly optimized, minor improvements possible), 4=Excellent (fast, lean, well-optimized)

### 3. Theming

**Check for**:
- **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change

**Score 0-4**: 0=No theming (hard-coded everything), 1=Minimal tokens (mostly hard-coded), 2=Partial (tokens exist but inconsistently used), 3=Good (tokens used, minor hard-coded values), 4=Excellent (full token system, dark mode works perfectly)

### 4. Responsive Design

**Check for**:
- **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants

**Score 0-4**: 0=Desktop-only (breaks on mobile), 1=Major issues (some breakpoints, many failures), 2=Partial (works on mobile, rough edges), 3=Good (responsive, minor touch target or overflow issues), 4=Excellent (fluid, all viewports, proper touch targets)

### 5. Anti-Patterns (CRITICAL)

Check against ALL the **DON'T** guidelines from the parent impeccable skill (already loaded in this context). Look for AI slop tells (AI color palette, gradient text, glassmorphism, hero metrics, card grids, generic fonts) and general design anti-patterns (gray on color, nested cards, bounce easing, redundant copy).

**Score 0-4**: 0=AI slop gallery (5+ tells), 1=Heavy AI aesthetic (3-4 tells), 2=Some tells (1-2 noticeable), 3=Mostly clean (subtle issues only), 4=No AI tells (distinctive, intentional design)

## Generate Report

### Audit Health Score

| # | Dimension | Score | Key Finding |
|---|-----------|-------|-------------|
| 1 | Accessibility | ? | [most critical a11y issue or "--"] |
| 2 | Performance | ? | |
| 3 | Responsive Design | ? | |
| 4 | Theming | ? | |
| 5 | Anti-Patterns | ? | |
| **Total** | | **??/20** | **[Rating band]** |

**Rating bands**: 18-20 Excellent (minor polish), 14-17 Good (address weak dimensions), 10-13 Acceptable (significant work needed), 6-9 Poor (major overhaul), 0-5 Critical (fundamental issues)

### Anti-Patterns Verdict
**Start here.** Pass/fail: Does this look AI-generated? List specific tells. Be brutally honest.

### Executive Summary
- Audit Health Score: **??/20** ([rating band])
- Total issues found (count by severity: P0/P1/P2/P3)
- Top 3-5 critical issues
- Recommended next steps

### Detailed Findings by Severity

Tag every issue with **P0-P3 severity**:
- **P0 Blocking**: Prevents task completion — fix immediately
- **P1 Major**: Significant difficulty or WCAG AA violation — fix before release
- **P2 Minor**: Annoyance, workaround exists — fix in next pass
- **P3 Polish**: Nice-to-fix, no real user impact — fix if time permits

For each issue, document:
- **[P?] Issue name**
- **Location**: Component, file, line
- **Category**: Accessibility / Performance / Theming / Responsive / Anti-Pattern
- **Impact**: How it affects users
- **WCAG/Standard**: Which standard it violates (if applicable)
- **Recommendation**: How to fix it
- **Suggested command**: Which command to use (prefer: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset)

### Patterns & Systemic Issues

Identify recurring problems that indicate systemic gaps rather than one-off mistakes:
- "Hard-coded colors appear in 15+ components, should use design tokens"
- "Touch targets consistently too small (<44px) throughout mobile experience"

### Positive Findings

Note what's working well — good practices to maintain and replicate.

## Recommended Actions

List recommended commands in priority order (P0 first, then P1, then P2):

1. **[P?] `$command-name`** — Brief description (specific context from audit findings)
2. **[P?] `$command-name`** — Brief description (specific context)

**Rules**: Only recommend commands from: $impeccable adapt, $impeccable animate, $impeccable audit, $impeccable bolder, $impeccable clarify, $impeccable colorize, $impeccable critique, $impeccable delight, $impeccable distill, $impeccable document, $impeccable harden, $impeccable layout, $impeccable onboard, $impeccable optimize, $impeccable overdrive, $impeccable polish, $impeccable quieter, $impeccable shape, $impeccable typeset. Map findings to the most appropriate command. End with `$impeccable polish` as the final step if any fixes were recommended.

After presenting the summary, tell the user:

> You can ask me to run these one at a time, all at once, or in any order you prefer.
>
> Re-run `$impeccable audit` after fixes to see your score improve.

**IMPORTANT**: Be thorough but actionable. Too many P3 issues creates noise. Focus on what actually matters.

**NEVER**:
- Report issues without explaining impact (why does this matter?)
- Provide generic recommendations (be specific and actionable)
- Skip positive findings (celebrate what works)
- Forget to prioritize (everything can't be P0)
- Report false positives without verification

Remember: You're a technical quality auditor. Document systematically, prioritize ruthlessly, cite specific code locations, and provide clear paths to improvement.

64:## Shared design laws
65-
66-Apply to every design, both registers. Match implementation complexity to the aesthetic vision — maximalism needs elaborate code, minimalism needs precision. Interpret creatively. Vary across projects; never converge on the same choices. GPT is capable of extraordinary work — don't hold back.
67-
68-### Color
69-
70-- Use OKLCH. Reduce chroma as lightness approaches 0 or 100 — high chroma at extremes looks garish.
71-- Never use `#000` or `#fff`. Tint every neutral toward the brand hue (chroma 0.005–0.01 is enough).
72-- Pick a **color strategy** before picking colors. Four steps on the commitment axis:
73-  - **Restrained** — tinted neutrals + one accent ≤10%. Product default; brand minimalism.
74-  - **Committed** — one saturated color carries 30–60% of the surface. Brand default for identity-driven pages.
75-  - **Full palette** — 3–4 named roles, each used deliberately. Brand campaigns; product data viz.
76-  - **Drenched** — the surface IS the color. Brand heroes, campaign pages.
77-- The "one accent ≤10%" rule is Restrained only. Committed / Full palette / Drenched exceed it on purpose. Don't collapse every design to Restrained by reflex.
78-
79-### Theme
80-
81-Dark vs. light is never a default. Not dark "because tools look cool dark." Not light "to be safe."
82-
83-Before choosing, write one sentence of physical scene: who uses this, where, under what ambient light, in what mood. If the sentence doesn't force the answer, it's not concrete enough — add detail until it does.
84-
85-"Observability dashboard" does not force an answer. "SRE glancing at incident severity on a 27-inch monitor at 2am in a dim room" does. Run the sentence, not the category.
86-
87-### Typography
88-
89-- Cap body line length at 65–75ch.
90-- Hierarchy through scale + weight contrast (≥1.25 ratio between steps). Avoid flat scales.
91-
92-### Layout
93-
94-- Vary spacing for rhythm. Same padding everywhere is monotony.
95-- Cards are the lazy answer. Use them only when they're truly the best affordance. Nested cards are always wrong.
96-- Don't wrap everything in a container. Most things don't need one.
97-
98-### Motion
99-
100-- Don't animate CSS layout properties.
101-- Ease out with exponential curves (ease-out-quart / quint / expo). No bounce, no elastic.
102-
103-### Absolute bans
104-
105-Match-and-refuse. If you're about to write any of these, rewrite the element with different structure.
106-
107-- **Side-stripe borders.** `border-left` or `border-right` greater than 1px as a colored accent on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. Never intentional. Rewrite with full borders, background tints, leading numbers/icons, or nothing.
108-- **Gradient text.** `background-clip: text` combined with a gradient background. Decorative, never meaningful. Use a single solid color. Emphasis via weight or size.
109-- **Glassmorphism as default.** Blurs and glass cards used decoratively. Rare and purposeful, or nothing.
110-- **The hero-metric template.** Big number, small label, supporting stats, gradient accent. SaaS cliché.
111-- **Identical card grids.** Same-sized cards with icon + heading + text, repeated endlessly.
112-- **Modal as first thought.** Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.
113-
114-### Copy
115-
116-- Every word earns its place. No restated headings, no intros that repeat the title.
117-- **No em dashes.** Use commas, colons, semicolons, periods, or parentheses. Also not `--`.
118-
119-### The AI slop test
120-
121-If someone could look at this interface and say "AI made that" without doubt, it's failed. Cross-register failures are the absolute bans above. Register-specific failures live in each reference.
122-
123-**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.

123-**Category-reflex check.** Run at two altitudes — the second one catches what the first one misses.
124-
125-- **First-order:** if someone could guess the theme + palette from the category alone — "observability → dark blue", "healthcare → white + teal", "finance → navy + gold", "crypto → neon on black" — it's the first training-data reflex. Rework the scene sentence and color strategy until the answer isn't obvious from the domain.
126-- **Second-order:** if someone could guess the aesthetic family from category-plus-anti-references — "AI workflow tool that's not SaaS-cream → editorial-typographic", "fintech that's not navy-and-gold → terminal-native dark mode" — it's the trap one tier deeper. The first reflex was avoided; the second wasn't. Rework until both answers are not obvious. The brand register's [reflex-reject aesthetic lanes](reference/brand.md) list catches the currently-saturated families.

97:## Accessibility & Inclusion
98-
99-**WCAG 2.1 AA is a binding floor across every surface.** It is a defect gate, not an aspiration. A finding below the AA bar is a bug and is fixed, not argued.
100-
101-- Text contrast is at least 4.5:1 against its actual rendered background, and at least 3:1 for large text and for the visual boundaries of interactive controls. Students read dense argument text on this platform for hours at a time.
102-- Every interactive element has a visible focus indicator.
103-- `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected. Motion is never load-bearing for understanding or for completing a task.
104-- Interactive targets meet the 44x44px minimum, and meet it by being that size rather than by a side effect such as wrapped text.
105-- Screen readers are informed of state that changes without a page load: unread counts, send confirmations, and live updates.
106-- Contrast is verified against what actually renders in the browser, not against the token values a theme is assumed to apply. The theme layer has shipped a light card on a near-black page while the token file read as correct.
====DESIGN
147-What they do share is restraint about meaning. In both, colour is a signal. Green, red and amber say something happened. Nothing is coloured to be warm, to look premium, or to fill a space. Depth comes from hairlines and background tone rather than shadow, and hierarchy comes from size, weight and position rather than hue.
148-
149:Inside the Workroom, individual rooms may commit fully to their own world. The eight Activity Centre worlds, the scoped bootcamps and the inbox each carry a painted identity behind a wrapper class. That is sanctioned and it is what an academy is: neutral corridors, characterful rooms. The corridor never takes on a room's paint.
150-
151-**Key Characteristics:**
152-- Two registers, one institution: a dark marble Hall at the door, a light neutral Workroom inside.
153-- Colour means something or it is absent.
154-- Flat surfaces, hairline separation, no drop shadows.
155-- Precise and quiet controls that stay out of the reader's way.
156-- Rooms may be worlds; corridors never are.
157-
158-## Colors
159-
160-Two grounds, one shared vocabulary of meaning.
161-
162-### Primary
163-
164-- **Workroom Ink** (`#121212`): the Workroom's near-black. It is the text colour, the fill of every primary button, and the colour of the focus ring. The Workroom has no accent, so ink carries emphasis by itself.
165-- **Hall Bronze** (`#c6a467`): the Hall's warm light. Hairline borders on the call to action, a sheen across it on hover, and the selection highlight. It exists only on the Hall's night ground and never crosses into the Workroom.
166-
167-### Secondary
168-
169-- **Hall Cyan** (`#8ad8e2`): the Hall's cool instrument note against the bronze, and the colour of its focus outlines. Rare by design; it marks precision where bronze marks warmth.
170-- **Teacher Amber** (`#c7841f`): the single accent of the teacher workspace, with a hue-locked ramp for hover (`#af7318`), text on tinted fills (`#895910`) and a light step (`#e0a648`). Deliberately theme-independent: the workspace is always the light format. It is the one accent in the product, and it belongs to the operator, not the student.
171-
172-### Tertiary
173-
174-Individual rooms carry their own palettes behind a wrapper class and are documented in their own surface briefs, not here. The wrappers in use are `.classroom-root`, `.mc-bootcamp`, `.ff-bootcamp`, `.rc-bootcamp`, `.topic-abstract`, `.cs-punk`, and the eight Activity Centre worlds.
175-
176:`.classroom-root` was added to this list on 2026-09-08, after an audit found the classroom running on Outfit and a warm palette while this record still described it as neutral Workroom chrome. It is a sanctioned world, not drift: the warmth and the typeface are deliberate and they are what makes the classroom feel like a study rather than a dashboard. The record was wrong, not the code. Its brief is `.impeccable/surfaces/classroom.md`. Note that this is the one sanctioned world that is also a corridor, so the rule below about corridors never being worlds now has exactly one documented exception.
177-
178-### Neutral
179-
180-- **Workroom Page** (`#fcfcfc`) and **Card** (`#ffffff`): the page ground and the surface that sits on it. One step of separation, no more.
181-- **Workroom Raised** (`#f7f7f7`) and **Fill** (`#f2f2f2`): the two tonal steps available for layering and for resting fills.
182-- **Workroom Ink Muted** (`#737373`): secondary text. Measures 4.62:1 on the page, which clears the floor with almost nothing to spare. Do not lighten it.
183-- **Workroom Hairline** (`#e0e0e0`) and **Field** (`#e6e6e6`): the borders that do the work shadows used to.
184-- **Hall Marble** (`#eae5da`), with secondary (`#a6a093`) and muted (`#6e695e`) steps: warm off-white text on the night ground.
185-
186-### State (semantic only, never decorative)
187-
188-- **Correct** (`#10b77f`) and **Incorrect** (`#f43e5c`): a student's attempt was right or wrong. Held under their own names, separate from system status, so a future shift to one cannot silently reframe the other.
189-- **Warn** (`#f59f0a`): a bonus, a stretch, a non-blocking notice.
190-- **Success** (`#21c45d`), **Warning** (`#f59f0a`), **Destructive** (`#dc2828`): system status, not student performance.
191-
192-### Named Rules
193-
194-**The Ground Decides Rule.** A warm colour is legible on the Hall's night and illegible on the Workroom's page. Never carry a colour across the seam on the strength of liking it; measure it against the ground it will land on first.
195-
196-**The Meaning-Only Rule.** A colour appears in the Workroom because something is right, wrong, cautionary, or dangerous. There is no fifth reason. If a surface feels flat and the impulse is to add a colour, the answer is type and space.
197-
198-**The Named Ground Rule.** No colour is written as a literal hex in a component. It resolves from a token, and a token that resolves to nothing is a defect, not a subtle effect. Three colour names in this codebase pointed at variables that were never defined, and the product silently lost its focus ring for five months.

200-## Typography

====SURFACE
45:## Read, Recall, Colour door (locked 2026-09-05)
46-
47-The two RRC doors (`SectionLibraryLaunch`, `SectionLaunch`, both drawn by `SectionLaunchFrame`) are a committed world of their own, mode **Persuade** at the top and **Operate** in the slab. Joshua's brief: a layered, high-end landing page in the spirit of heylemon.ai (cream ground, painted panels, serif display with italic emphasis, mono micro-labels, a dictation demo) with fora.so's depth-plane hero, and bold step 1 to 4 instructions.
48-
49-- **World:** one dusk. The runner's indigo (`#1a1635`) lifted into a sky over a painted meadow, cream paper (`#f6f3ea`) beneath. Display is Fraunces at opsz 144; body Inter; labels IBM Plex Mono. Marigold marks the Colour step, emerald marks a carried detail, nothing else is coloured.
50-- **Hero:** `MeadowScene` paints five depth planes generatively in SVG (seeded ridges, seeded grass blades, feTurbulence grain) so no raster ships; each plane rides `useScroll` at its own rate and the foreground grass crosses in front of the demo card. The headline blurs and recedes on the same scroll value.
51-- **Signature moment:** `RecallDemo`, a looping performance of the method on a synthetic stimulus, labelled as such, whose text literally dissolves at the Recall step.
52-- **Steps:** four cards, each with a small drawn scene, doubling as the ARIA tablist the tests already contract (`sl-beat`, `sl-beat-tab-N`, `sl-beat-panel`). The full briefing is the second tab of the drawer.
53-- **Tokens:** scoped to `.sl` as `--rd-*`. The design detector reports them as outside DESIGN.md; that is the sanctioned-world exception above, not drift.
54-
55-## The Report Card (locked 2026-09-08)
56-
57-Its own world, scoped to `.rc` in `report-card.css`. Joshua's brief: highly
58-organised, easy to run through, high-end, with her recording and the standard's
59-side by side and the transcript following the sound. Explicitly **not cream, not
60-emerald, and not grey** ("the cream paper colour is overdone and too AI
61-looking", "I don't want emerald", "it should be much more colorful"). The second
62-sanctioned departure from the wing's surface after the RRC door.
63-
64-- **Two grounds.** A deep plum console (`oklch(0.245 0.058 312)`) holds the
65-  verdict, the transport and the ledger; a violet-cast white page
66-  (`oklch(0.979 0.008 312)`) holds everything she reads at length.
67-- **Colour means a KIND of feedback, never a severity.** The mechanic is
68-  Grammarly's, which colours by category and reserves red for outright errors.
69-  Four fixed tones: **Detail** plum-rose, **Read** teal, **Colour** gold,
70-  **Method** indigo. A miss is the colour of the thing it is a miss of, which is
71-  what stops twenty-two stimuli of corrections reading as a heat map of failure.
72-- **Signal magenta is sound, not a category.** It marks the line currently
73-  playing and nothing else, held at hue 328 so it separates from the
74-  plum-rose dropped chips it shares a line with.
75-- **Hues chosen from research, not taste** (Firecrawl, 2026-09-08). Saturated
76-  right now: acid yellow, electric cobalt, orange and coral, warm sand and
77-  terracotta, black plus one neon accent. Under-used: violet and plum, soft
78-  cyan, dusty rose as a field, complex cool neutrals. Green and cream are the
79-  owner's veto; orange-red was this file's first answer and is on the over-used
80-  list. The neutrals are tinted toward violet the way Material 3's baseline is
81-  (`#fffbfe` paper, `#7d5260` plum tertiary), a system built to hold AA.
82-- **Band is fill, not hue.** The ledger's marks differ by how full they are, in
83-  gold; a recital is dashed and a lost one is a hollow rose ring. It survives
84-  grayscale, which is what lets category own the colour.
85-- **One stimulus at a time.** Twenty-two accordion rows became a ledger of
86-  twenty-two marks plus one open stimulus in a fixed order: the passage, what
87-  you held, what slipped, your Read, line by line, what you said, next time.
88-- **No Detail arithmetic on screen.** A Detail is our grading unit and she has
89-  never seen one enumerated, so "20 of 21 details" is gone: `howMuch()` says
90-  "Almost all of it came back" instead (Joshua, 2026-09-08).
91-- **Contrast is measured, not assumed.** `scripts/_rc_contrast.mjs` converts
92-  computed colours through a canvas (Chrome serialises them as `oklch()`) and
93-  every text node clears 4.5:1 on its own ground, in the default, playing and
94-  clean states.
95-- **Type ramp:** Fraunces display, Lexend body, IBM Plex Mono labels. The design
96-  detector reports the sizes as outside DESIGN.md; that is this sanctioned world,
97-  not drift, the same exception the RRC door's `--rd-*` tokens carry.
98-- **Harness:** `/src/dev/report-card-preview.html` renders it at twenty-two
99-  stimuli without `App.tsx`. `?state=clean|faults|partial`, `?playing=N` to
100-  freeze the playhead on a line. `scripts/_rc_shots.mjs` captures the matrix.

Tool result:
# Read, Recall, Colour section briefing page: design critique

Date: 2026-09-08. Method: `$impeccable critique` on
`src/features/activity-centre/components/SectionBriefingPage.tsx`, two isolated
passes (a design review with live desktop inspection, and the deterministic
detector) woven into one report. Related: [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]], [[ASSIGN-AN-ACTIVITY]].

## Design health score

| # | Heuristic | Score | Key issue |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Visibility of system status | 2 | "One take, no pause" and "about 25 minutes" are not on the page |
| 2 | Match system / real world | 3 | "The Recall." reads as a typo beside three imperatives |
| 3 | User control and freedom | 3 | Back is 44x44 and works; nothing else needed |
| 4 | Consistency and standards | 2 | Three micro-label styles; the two H2s render at different weights |
| 5 | Error prevention | 3 | No warning that the browser will ask for the microphone |
| 6 | Recognition rather than recall | 3 | A " +" text glyph is the only disclosure affordance |
| 7 | Flexibility and efficiency | 2 | Space is the runner's only key but does nothing here |
| 8 | Aesthetic and minimalist | 3 | Chip and H1 both say the product name; foot restates the notice |
| 9 | Error recovery | 2 | Mic-denied case has no hint on this page |
| 10 | Help and documentation | 4 | The collapsed long version is exactly right |
| **Total** | | **27/40** | **Acceptable, upper end** |

## Anti-patterns verdict

Does it look AI-made? No. Single column, four numbered steps, one primary
action, Fraunces title on cream, no hero metric, no icon grid, no gradient text,
no side stripes, no em dashes in the rendered DOM. The one drift toward generic
is the Start button: a landing-page pill with a hover lift and drop shadow,
written for the dark hero and inherited by this cream task page.

Deterministic scan: `npx impeccable --json` on the page returned zero findings.
The directory scan of `components/` returned 53 advisory findings, none touching
this page or `section-launch.css` (47 are in `activity-scenes.css`). No false
positives to triage. Browser overlay could not run: impeccable 4.0.4 ships no
`detect.js`, and the page has no CSP, so this is a tooling gap and not a page
problem. Cognitive load: 1 of 8 checks failed (working memory), which is low.

## Overall impression

The structure is right and the copy is the best on the platform. The misses are
content gaps and vocabulary drift, not layout. One of them matters: the page was
edited on 2026-09-07 on the belief that the one-take fact survived in Step 4.
It did not. It lives in `instructions.rules[0]`, which this page never renders.

## What's working

- A page, not a modal, with all four steps open and numbered. "Laughing is
  fine." and "It does not have to be clever, it has to be yours." earn their place.
- The hero classes are honestly re-grounded for paper, and the comments say why.
- Accessibility basics hold, measured live: H1 H2 H2 H3, Back 44x44, 2px focus
  rings, no live-region noise.

## Priority issues

**[P1] The two facts she most needs before Start are not on the page.**
Verified live with the long version open: zero hits for "one take"; "minute"
only inside the collapsed essay's sixth heading. The code comment at
`SectionBriefingPage.tsx:63` claims Step 4 carries it. Step 4 does not.
Fix: one plain sentence beside the foot Start: "It runs in one take, about
twenty-five minutes, and it does not pause." Or render `briefing.closer`,
which the type describes as "the last beat before the Start action" and which
nothing renders. Correct the comment. Command: `$impeccable clarify`.

**[P1] The calming notice is styled as a fifth step card.**
`.sl-notice` and `.sl-bp-list li` share fill, hairline, and radius. The one
sentence written to lower her shoulders reads as procedure.
Fix: take the box off it. A plain ink line under the CTA. Command: `$impeccable layout`.

**[P2] Step body lines run about 105 characters.** The essay caps at 68ch; the
step list has no measure. Fix: `max-width: 62ch` on `.sl-bp-list li p` and
`.sl-notice`. Command: `$impeccable typeset`.

**[P2] Three micro-label styles on one page, and mismatched H2s.** Mono chip,
Inter-uppercase h2, Inter-uppercase step number, while `.sl-label` is declared
the one style and goes unused. The summary's h2 inherits body weight, so the
only help on the page looks like a stray sentence. Fix: put step numbers and
h2s on `.sl-label`; give the summary the same h2 treatment and a real chevron.
Command: `$impeccable typeset`.

**[P2] The lede is silently overridden.** `.sl-essay p` outranks `.rd-lede`, so
the 20px italic lede renders at 16.5px. Fix: `.sl-essay p.rd-lede`.
Command: `$impeccable polish`.

**[P3] Landing-page button on a task page.** Drop the transform and outer
shadow on `.sl-briefing-page .sl-start`; keep the fill darkening.
Command: `$impeccable quieter`.

## Persona red flags

- **Jordan (first-timer):** reads "Press space" in Step 4 and looks for a Space
  button; presses Start and meets a microphone permission dialog with no warning.
- **Sam (screen reader):** an `h2` nested inside `summary` is exposed
  inconsistently; some readers drop it from the headings list, the very outline
  the h2 was added to fix.
- **Casey (mobile):** the reassurance sits at the top and the Start she presses
  is 1,000px below it. Same fix as the first P1. Mobile was reasoned from the
  CSS, not seen: the viewport resize did not take.
- **A coaching client at 9pm:** wants "how long is this and can I stop if my
  roommate walks in." The page answers neither, and nothing on it is addressed
  from her coach.

## Minor observations

- Chip and H1 both say "Read, Recall, Colour"; the chip is redundant here.
- The head's side-by-side layout never fits at 840px, so the CTA always wraps
  under the tagline. What ships is accidental.
- Empty tagline still renders an empty `p` with top margin.
- Foot copy repeats "begins" and restates the notice's fact.
- Summary focus ring is `--rd-ink-2`, buttons are `--rd-ink`.
- `.sl-bp-meta` is defined and unused.

## Questions to consider

1. Why is the one sentence written to calm her styled identically to the four things she has to do?
2. If the runner's only key is Space, what would happen if Space started the section from here?
3. What would be missing if the page were only the notice, the four steps, and one Start at the foot?

## Recommended order

1. `$impeccable clarify`: the one-take and 25-minute sentence at the foot, render `closer`, fix the comment.
2. `$impeccable layout`: un-box the notice.
3. `$impeccable typeset`: 62ch measure, one label style, matching H2s, lede fix.
4. `$impeccable quieter`: the Start button's lift and shadow.
5. `$impeccable polish` last.

    18	 * Pressing Start on a section used to open the microphone straight away, and

    20	 * page is the whole briefing laid out in order, every step numbered and open,
    21	 * with one Start button at the top and the same one at the foot. The written
    22	 * instructions sit under it, closed. Nothing here begins the run.

    37	      <ArrowRight size={16} strokeWidth={2.4} aria-hidden="true" />

    39	  );
    40	
    41	  return (
    42	    <div className="sl sl-briefing-page">
    43	      <header className="sl-bp-head">
    44	        <div>

     1	/* The Read, Recall, Colour door.
     2	 *
     3	 * One evening, in two registers. The hero is the runner's own indigo lifted
     4	 * into a dusk sky over a painted meadow, cut into depth planes that move at
     5	 * their own rates as she scrolls; everything under it sits on cream paper in
     6	 * the same light. The method is performed before it is explained, the four
     7	 * steps are the next thing she meets, and the sections she can run sit
     8	 * directly beneath.
     9	 *
    10	 * Every value is scoped to `.sl`. Nothing here touches the runner
    11	 * (`recall/section.css`) or the shell outside this door. Class names beginning
    12	 * `sl-` are the door's contract with its tests and its two wrappers; `rd-`
    13	 * names are the design and may change freely.
    14	 */
    15	
    16	.sl {

    19	  --rd-on-dark: #fff;

    33	  --rd-marigold: #f2b544;

   230	  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 144, "SOFT" 30, "WONK" 0;
   231	  font-size: clamp(46px, 6.6vw, 96px);
   232	  line-height: 0.98;
   233	  letter-spacing: -0.03em;
   234	  text-wrap: balance;
   235	  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
   236	}
   237	.sl-title em {
   238	  font-weight: 300;
   239	  color: var(--rd-apricot-light);
   240	}
   241	.sl-tagline {
   242	  margin: 22px auto 0;
   243	  max-width: 54ch;
   244	  font-size: clamp(16px, 1.3vw, 18px);
   245	  line-height: 1.55;
   246	  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
   247	  text-shadow: 0 1px 2px rgba(22, 19, 43, 0.35);
   248	  text-wrap: pretty;
   249	}
   250	.sl-tagline strong {
   251	  font-weight: 600;
   252	  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
   253	}
   254	.sl-cta {
   255	  display: flex;
   256	  justify-content: center;
   257	  align-items: center;
   258	  gap: 12px 22px;
   259	  flex-wrap: wrap;
   260	  margin-top: 30px;
   261	}
   262	.sl-start {
   263	  appearance: none;
   264	  border: 0;
   265	  cursor: pointer;
   266	  display: inline-flex;
   267	  align-items: center;
   268	  gap: 10px;
   269	  min-height: 52px;
   270	  padding: 0 26px;
   271	  font: inherit;
   272	  font-size: 15px;
   273	  font-weight: 600;
   274	  letter-spacing: -0.005em;
   275	  color: var(--rd-cream);
   276	  background: var(--rd-ink);
   277	  border-radius: 9999px;
   278	  box-shadow:
   279	    0 0 0 1px rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.12),
   280	    0 18px 30px -18px rgba(22, 19, 43, 0.7);
   281	  transition:
   282	    transform 0.35s var(--rd-ease),
   283	    background 0.2s ease,
   284	    box-shadow 0.35s var(--rd-ease);
   285	}
   286	.sl-start:hover {
   287	  background: var(--rd-indigo-2);
   288	  transform: translateY(-2px);
   289	  box-shadow:
   290	    0 0 0 1px rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.16),
   291	    0 24px 36px -18px rgba(22, 19, 43, 0.75);
   292	}
   293	.sl-start:active {
   294	  transform: translateY(0);
   295	}
   296	.sl-start svg {
   297	  transition: transform 0.35s var(--rd-ease);
   298	}
   299	.sl-start:hover svg {
   300	  transform: translateY(2px);
   301	}
   302	.sl-brief {
   303	  appearance: none;
   304	  border: 0;
   305	  background: none;
   306	  cursor: pointer;
   307	  display: inline-flex;
   308	  align-items: center;
   309	  gap: 7px;
   310	  min-height: 44px;
   311	  padding: 0 4px;
   312	  font: inherit;
   313	  font-size: 15px;
   314	  font-weight: 500;
   315	  color: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.92);
   316	  text-decoration: underline;
   317	  text-decoration-color: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.4);
   318	  text-underline-offset: 5px;
   319	  transition: text-decoration-color 0.2s ease;
   320	}
   321	.sl-brief:hover {
   322	  text-decoration-color: var(--rd-on-dark);
   323	}
   324	.sl-brief svg {
   325	  transition: transform 0.35s var(--rd-ease);
   326	}
   327	.sl-brief:hover svg {
   328	  transform: translateX(3px);
   329	}
   330	.rd-hero :focus-visible {
   331	  outline-color: var(--rd-on-dark);
   332	}
   333	
   334	/* Reserves the room the stage lives in, so the copy never sits on it. */
   335	.rd-hero-space {
   336	  flex: 1 0 auto;
   337	  min-height: clamp(470px, 52vh, 600px);
   338	}
   339	
   340	/* =============================================================== demo */
   341	
   342	.rd-demo {
   343	  width: min(800px, 100%);
   344	  background: var(--rd-paper);
   345	  color: var(--rd-ink);
   346	  border-radius: 18px;
   347	  overflow: hidden;
   348	  text-align: left;
   349	  box-shadow:
   350	    0 0 0 1px rgba(22, 19, 43, 0.1),
   351	    0 40px 70px -34px rgba(22, 19, 43, 0.65),
   352	    0 12px 24px -16px rgba(22, 19, 43, 0.4);
   353	}
   354	.rd-demo-bar {
   355	  display: flex;
   356	  align-items: center;
   357	  gap: 12px;
   358	  padding: 10px 14px;
   359	  background: #fbf9f3;
   360	  border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   361	}
   362	.rd-demo-dots {
   363	  display: inline-flex;
   364	  gap: 6px;
   365	}
   366	.rd-demo-dots i {
   367	  width: 9px;
   368	  height: 9px;
   369	  border-radius: 50%;
   370	  background: #e0ddd3;
   371	}
   372	.rd-demo-dots i:first-child {
   373	  background: #f0876f;
   374	}
   375	.rd-demo-dots i:nth-child(2) {
   376	  background: #f2c65a;
   377	}
   378	.rd-demo-dots i:nth-child(3) {
   379	  background: #7fcb8e;
   380	}
   381	.rd-demo-title {
   382	  font-size: 13px;
   383	  font-weight: 600;
   384	  letter-spacing: -0.01em;
   385	}
   386	.rd-demo-step {
   387	  margin-left: auto;
   388	  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
   389	  font-size: 10.5px;
   390	  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
   391	  text-transform: uppercase;
   392	  color: var(--rd-marigold-ink);
   393	  background: var(--rd-marigold-wash);
   394	  border-radius: 9999px;
   395	  padding: 4px 10px;
   396	  white-space: nowrap;
   397	}
   398	.rd-demo-rec {
   399	  display: inline-flex;
   400	  align-items: center;
   401	  gap: 7px;
   402	  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
   403	  font-size: 11.5px;
   404	  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
   405	  font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums;
   406	}
   407	.rd-rec-dot {
   408	  width: 7px;
   409	  height: 7px;
   410	  border-radius: 50%;
   411	  background: #d2402e;
   412	  opacity: 0.45;
   413	}
   414	.rd-rec-dot[data-live] {
   415	  opacity: 1;
   416	  animation: rd-blink 1.3s ease-in-out infinite;
   417	}
   418	@keyframes rd-blink {
   419	  50% {
   420	    opacity: 0.25;
   421	  }
   422	}
   423	.rd-demo-play {
   424	  appearance: none;
   425	  cursor: pointer;
   426	  display: inline-flex;
   427	  align-items: center;
   428	  justify-content: center;
   429	  width: 28px;
   430	  height: 28px;
   431	  border-radius: 50%;
   432	  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   433	  background: var(--rd-paper);
   434	  color: var(--rd-ink);
   435	  padding: 0;
   436	  transition: background 0.2s ease;
   437	}
   438	.rd-demo-play:hover:not(:disabled) {
   439	  background: var(--rd-paper-2);
   440	}
   441	.rd-demo-play:disabled {
   442	  opacity: 0.4;
   443	  cursor: default;
   444	}
   445	.rd-demo-body {
   446	  display: grid;
   447	  grid-template-columns: 1.12fr 1fr;
   448	}
   449	.rd-paper {
   450	  position: relative;
   451	  display: flex;
   452	  flex-direction: column;
   453	  min-height: 262px;
   454	  padding: 18px 20px 16px;
   455	  border-right: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   456	}
   457	.rd-demo-label {
   458	  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
   459	  font-size: 10px;
   460	  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
   461	  text-transform: uppercase;
   462	  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
   463	}
   464	.rd-stim {
   465	  margin: 12px 0 0;
   466	  font-family: var(--rd-display);
   467	  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 18;
   468	  font-size: 16.5px;
   469	  line-height: 1.55;
   470	  color: var(--rd-ink);
   471	  transition:
   472	    opacity 0.9s var(--rd-ease),
   473	    filter 0.9s var(--rd-ease),
   474	    transform 0.9s var(--rd-ease);
   475	}
   476	.rd-paper[data-gone] .rd-stim {
   477	  opacity: 0;
   478	  filter: blur(10px);
   479	  transform: translateY(-6px);
   480	}
   481	.rd-stim-line {
   482	  border-radius: 4px;
   483	  padding: 1px 2px;
   484	  box-decoration-break: clone;
   485	  -webkit-box-decoration-break: clone;
   486	  transition:
   487	    background 0.45s ease,
   488	    color 0.45s ease;
   489	}
   490	.rd-stim-line[data-active] {
   491	  background: var(--rd-marigold-wash);
   492	}
   493	.rd-stim-line[data-done] {
   494	  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
   495	}
   496	.rd-gone {
   497	  position: absolute;
   498	  left: 20px;
   499	  right: 20px;
   500	  top: 46%;
   501	  transform: translateY(-50%);
   502	  margin: 0;
   503	  padding: 0 12px;
   504	  text-align: center;
   505	  font-family: var(--rd-display);
   506	  font-style: italic;
   507	  font-size: 17px;
   508	  line-height: 1.4;
   509	  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
   510	}
   511	.rd-keyrow {
   512	  display: flex;
   513	  align-items: center;
   514	  gap: 10px;
   515	  margin-top: auto;
   516	  padding-top: 16px;
   517	}
   518	.rd-key {
   519	  display: inline-block;
   520	  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
   521	  font-size: 11px;
   522	  letter-spacing: 0.06em;
   523	  padding: 4px 13px;
   524	  border-radius: 7px;
   525	  background: var(--rd-on-dark);
   526	  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
   527	  box-shadow:
   528	    0 2px 0 #d9d3c6,
   529	    0 0 0 1px var(--rd-line);
   530	  transition:
   531	    transform 0.12s ease,
   532	    box-shadow 0.12s ease,
   533	    background 0.12s ease;
   534	}
   535	.rd-key[data-pressed] {
   536	  transform: translateY(2px);
   537	  background: var(--rd-marigold-wash);
   538	  box-shadow:
   539	    0 0 0 #d9d3c6,
   540	    0 0 0 1px #cfc9bd;
   541	}
   542	.rd-keyhint {
   543	  font-size: 12px;
   544	  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
   545	}
   546	.rd-voice {
   547	  display: flex;
   548	  flex-direction: column;
   549	  min-height: 262px;
   550	  padding: 18px 20px;
   551	  background: #f4f1ea;
   552	}
   553	.rd-voice-stack {
   554	  display: flex;
   555	  flex-direction: column;
   556	  gap: 10px;
   557	  flex: 1;
   558	  margin-top: 12px;
   559	}
   560	.rd-bubble {
   561	  background: var(--rd-indigo);
   562	  color: var(--rd-on-dark);
   563	  border-radius: 16px 16px 16px 5px;
   564	  padding: 11px 14px 13px;
   565	  font-size: 14px;
   566	  line-height: 1.5;
   567	  box-shadow: 0 14px 24px -18px rgba(22, 19, 43, 0.7);
   568	}
   569	.rd-bubble p {
   570	  margin: 6px 0 0;
   571	}
   572	.rd-bubble-head {
   573	  display: flex;
   574	  align-items: center;
   575	  gap: 8px;
   576	  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
   577	  font-size: 10.5px;
   578	  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
   579	  text-transform: uppercase;
   580	  color: var(--rd-apricot-light);
   581	}
   582	.rd-wave {
   583	  display: inline-flex;
   584	  align-items: center;
   585	  gap: 2px;
   586	  height: 12px;
   587	}
   588	.rd-wave i {
   589	  display: block;
   590	  width: 2px;
   591	  height: 4px;
   592	  border-radius: 1px;
   593	  background: currentColor;
   594	}
   595	.rd-wave i:nth-child(2n) {
   596	  height: 8px;
   597	}
   598	.rd-wave i:nth-child(4) {
   599	  height: 11px;
   600	}
   601	.rd-wave[data-live] i {
   602	  animation: rd-wave 0.85s ease-in-out infinite alternate;
   603	}
   604	@keyframes rd-wave {
   605	  from {
   606	    height: 3px;
   607	  }
   608	  to {
   609	    height: 12px;
   610	  }
   611	}
   612	.rd-caret {
   613	  display: inline-block;
   614	  width: 2px;
   615	  height: 1em;
   616	  margin-left: 1px;
   617	  vertical-align: -2px;
   618	  background: var(--rd-apricot-light);
   619	  animation: rd-caret 1s steps(2) infinite;
   620	}
   621	@keyframes rd-caret {
   622	  50% {
   623	    opacity: 0;
   624	  }
   625	}
   626	.rd-colour {
   627	  align-self: flex-end;
   628	  max-width: 90%;
   629	  background: var(--rd-marigold);
   630	  color: var(--rd-ink);
   631	  border-radius: 14px 14px 5px 14px;
   632	  padding: 9px 14px 11px;
   633	  font-family: var(--rd-display);
   634	  font-style: italic;
   635	  font-size: 15.5px;
   636	  line-height: 1.4;
   637	  box-shadow: 0 12px 22px -16px rgba(110, 70, 0, 0.6);
   638	}
   639	.rd-colour p {
   640	  margin: 4px 0 0;
   641	}
   642	.rd-colour-head {
   643	  display: block;
   644	  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
   645	  font-style: normal;
   646	  font-size: 10px;
   647	  letter-spacing: 0.14em;
   648	  text-transform: uppercase;
   649	  color: var(--rd-marigold-ink);
   650	}
   651	.rd-bubble-recall {
   652	  border-radius: 16px 16px 16px 5px;
   653	}
   654	.rd-tally {
   655	  background: var(--rd-paper);
   656	  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   657	  border-radius: 14px;
   658	  padding: 12px 14px 13px;
   659	}
   660	.rd-tally-head {
   661	  display: block;
   662	  font-size: 13px;
   663	  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
   664	}
   665	.rd-tally-head strong {
   666	  font-family: var(--rd-display);
   667	  font-weight: 500;
   668	  font-size: 20px;
   669	  color: var(--rd-ink);
   670	  margin-right: 2px;
   671	}
   672	.rd-tally-list {
   673	  list-style: none;
   674	  margin: 9px 0 0;
   675	  padding: 0;
   676	  display: flex;
   677	  flex-wrap: wrap;
   678	  gap: 6px;
   679	}
   680	.rd-tally-list li {
   681	  font-size: 12px;
   682	  padding: 3px 9px;
   683	  border-radius: 9999px;
   684	  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   685	  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
   686	}
   687	.rd-tally-list li[data-carried] {
   688	  background: var(--rd-emerald-wash);
   689	  border-color: transparent;
   690	  color: var(--rd-emerald);
   691	  font-weight: 500;
   692	}
   693	.rd-quiet {
   694	  margin: 6px 0 0;
   695	  font-family: var(--rd-display);
   696	  font-style: italic;
   697	  font-size: 15px;
   698	  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
   699	}
   700	.rd-demo-foot {
   701	  margin: 0;
   702	  padding: 8px 14px;
   703	  border-top: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   704	  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
   705	  font-size: 10px;
   706	  letter-spacing: 0.08em;
   707	  text-transform: uppercase;
   708	  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
   709	}
   710	
   711	/* =============================================================== band */
   712	
   713	.rd-band {
   714	  display: grid;
   715	  gap: 14px;
   716	  max-width: 1240px;
   717	  margin: 28px auto 0;
   718	  padding: 0 24px;
   719	}
   720	.sl-notice {
   721	  margin: 0;
   722	  padding: 16px 20px;
   723	  background: var(--rd-paper);
   724	  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   725	  border-radius: 16px;
   726	  font-size: 15px;
   727	  line-height: 1.55;
   728	  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
   729	}
   730	
   731	/* ============================================================= method */
   732	
   733	.rd-method,
   734	.rd-counts,
   735	.rd-slab {
   736	  max-width: 1240px;
   737	  margin: 0 auto;
   738	  padding: 104px 24px 0;
   739	}
   740	.rd-method-head,
   741	.rd-counts-head {
   742	  display: flex;
   743	  align-items: flex-end;
   744	  justify-content: space-between;
   745	  gap: 24px 48px;
   746	  flex-wrap: wrap;
   747	  margin-bottom: 36px;
   748	}
   749	.rd-h2 {
   750	  margin: 0;
   751	  font-family: var(--rd-display);
   752	  font-weight: 400;
   753	  font-variation-settings: "opsz" 144, "SOFT" 30;
   754	  font-size: clamp(34px, 4.4vw, 60px);
   755	  line-height: 1.02;
   756	  letter-spacing: -0.028em;
   757	  text-wrap: balance;
   758	  color: var(--rd-ink);
   759	}
   760	.rd-h2 em {
   761	  font-weight: 300;
   762	}
   763	.rd-method-sub {
   764	  margin: 0;
   765	  max-width: 36ch;
   766	  font-size: 17px;
   767	  line-height: 1.5;
   768	  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
   769	  text-wrap: pretty;
   770	}
   771	
   772	.sl-beats {
   773	  display: grid;
   774	  grid-template-columns: repeat(var(--sl-beat-count, 4), minmax(0, 1fr));
   775	  gap: 16px;
   776	}
   777	.sl-beat {
   778	  appearance: none;
   779	  cursor: pointer;
   780	  display: flex;
   781	  flex-direction: column;
   782	  text-align: left;
   783	  font: inherit;
   784	  color: inherit;
   785	  padding: 0 0 26px;
   786	  background: var(--rd-paper);
   787	  border: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   788	  border-radius: var(--rd-radius);
   789	  overflow: hidden;
   790	  transition:
   791	    transform 0.5s var(--rd-ease),
   792	    box-shadow 0.5s var(--rd-ease),
   793	    border-color 0.2s ease;
   794	}
   795	.sl-beat:hover {
   796	  transform: translateY(-4px);
   797	  box-shadow: 0 30px 44px -30px rgba(22, 19, 43, 0.4);
   798	}
   799	.sl-beat[aria-selected="true"] {
   800	  border-color: var(--rd-ink);
   801	  box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 1px var(--rd-ink);
   802	}
   803	.rd-step-n {
   804	  margin: 22px 24px 0;
   805	  font-family: var(--rd-mono);
   806	  font-size: 11px;
   807	  letter-spacing: 0.16em;
   808	  text-transform: uppercase;
   809	  color: var(--rd-ink-3);
   810	}
   811	.sl-beat .sl-l {
   812	  display: block;
   813	  margin: 8px 24px 0;
   814	  font-family: var(--rd-display);
   815	  font-weight: 400;
   816	  font-size: 28px;
   817	  line-height: 1.1;
   818	  letter-spacing: -0.02em;
   819	  color: var(--rd-ink);
   820	}
   821	.sl-beat .sl-d {
   822	  display: -webkit-box;
   823	  -webkit-box-orient: vertical;
   824	  -webkit-line-clamp: 4;
   825	  overflow: hidden;
   826	  margin: 10px 24px 0;
   827	  font-size: 14.5px;
   828	  line-height: 1.55;
   829	  color: var(--rd-ink-2);
   830	}
   831	
   832	/* The four small scenes. */
   833	.rd-scene {
   834	  position: relative;
   835	  height: 172px;
   836	  overflow: hidden;
   837	  border-bottom: 1px solid var(--rd-line);
   838	}
   839	.rd-sc-read {
   840	  background: linear-gradient(180deg, #d6cff0 0%, #ecd8d8 60%, #f5d9be 100%);
   841	}
   842	.rd-sc-tell {
   843	  background: linear-gradient(180deg, #2a2450 0%, #5b4f8f 70%, #a89fe0 100%);
   844	}
   845	.rd-sc-colour {
   846	  background: linear-gradient(180deg, #f5d9be 0%, #f3c9a0 55%, #e8c9d3 100%);
   847	}
   848	.rd-sc-recall {
   849	  background: linear-gradient(180deg, #b9b0dd 0%, #d8cfe6 60%, #eadfd8 100%);
   850	}
   851	.rd-sc-page {
   852	  position: absolute;
   853	  left: 26px;
   854	  right: 26px;
   855	  bottom: -14px;
   856	  height: 128px;
   857	  display: flex;
   858	  flex-direction: column;
   859	  gap: 13px;
   860	  padding: 22px 20px;
   861	  background: var(--rd-paper);

50	              {instructions.tagline ? renderInlineMarks(instructions.tagline, "sl-bp-tag") : subtitle}
51	            </p>
52	          )}
53	        </div>
54	        <div className="sl-cta">
55	          {startButton("Start the section")}
56	          {onExit && (
57	            <button type="button" className="sl-brief" onClick={onExit}>
58	              Back
59	            </button>
60	          )}
61	        </div>
62	      </header>
63	
64	      {/* One fact, the one that lowers her shoulders before she reads. That the
65	          section records in one take with no pause is said at the foot, beside
66	          the Start she presses after reading; saying it here as well made the
67	          notice a paragraph she skipped. (An earlier version of this comment
68	          claimed Step 4 carried it. It did not: the sentence lived only in
69	          `instructions.rules`, which this page never renders, so for a day the
70	          page did not say it anywhere.) */}
71	      {/* No role="status". A live region is for state that changes without a page
72	          load; this sentence is the same every time, and announcing it as an
73	          update is noise. */}
74	      <p className="sl-notice">
75	        Nothing is recording yet. The microphone opens when you press Start.
76	      </p>
77	
78	      {steps.length > 0 && (
79	        <section className="sl-bp-steps" aria-label="What you do, step by step">
80	          <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">What you do, step by step</h2>
81	          <ol className="sl-bp-list">
82	            {steps.map((step, i) => (
83	              <li key={i} style={{ "--i": i } as React.CSSProperties}>
84	                <span className="sl-bp-n">Step {i + 1}</span>
85	                <strong>{renderInlineMarks(step.label, `sl-bps-l-${i}`)}</strong>
86	                <p>{renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bps-b-${i}`)}</p>
87	              </li>
88	            ))}
89	          </ol>
90	        </section>
91	      )}
92	
93	      {/* The four steps above ARE the instruction. This is the same method told
94	          again at length, and rendering both open left her reading the method
95	          twice with no way to tell which one she was meant to follow. Closed by
96	          default, one click from anyone who wants the long version. */}
97	      {briefing && (
98	        <details className="sl-bp-more">
99	          {/* The summary carries a real heading rather than only looking like one.

102	          <summary>
103	            <h2 className="sl-bp-h2">Read the long version</h2>
104	          </summary>
105	          <section className="sl-essay sl-bp-essay" aria-label="Full instructions">
106	          {briefing.lede && <p className="rd-lede">{renderInlineMarks(briefing.lede, "sl-bp-lede")}</p>}
107	          {briefing.sections.map((section, i) => (
108	            <section key={i}>
109	              <h3>{renderInlineMarks(section.heading, `sl-bp-h-${i}`)}</h3>
110	              {section.body.map((para, j) => (

113	              {section.steps && (

116	                    <li key={j}>

118	                      {renderInlineMarks(step.body, `sl-bp-sb-${i}-${j}`)}

121	                </ol>

128	                    </li>
129	                  ))}
130	                </ol>
131	              )}
132	            </section>
133	          ))}
134	          </section>
135	        </details>
136	      )}
137	
138	      {/* The two facts she needs at the moment she presses Start, said where
139	          she presses it: it does not pause, and what the time is for. The
140	          closer is the authored last beat before Start and belongs here too. */}
141	      <footer className="sl-bp-foot">
142	        <p className="sl-bp-fact">
143	          It runs in one take and does not pause. Twenty-five minutes for the whole section is
144	          the goal you are working toward, not a limit.

155	        </p>

157	      </footer>
158	    </div>
159	  );

5	
6	export interface SectionLibraryLaunchProps {
7	  title: string;
8	  subtitle: string | null;
9	  instructions: ActivityInstructions;
10	  /** The list itself, which owns the sections and the starting. */
11	  children: ReactNode;
12	  onExit?: () => void;
13	}
14	
15	/**
16	 * The door to Read, Recall, Colour: the method, and the sections it is run on.
17	 *
18	 * One drill, and every section reachable from the page that explains it. Before
19	 * this there were three screens between her and a run — an editorial intro
20	 * written for the typed Activities, a bare list, and then the section's own
21	 * page — and only the last of them was designed for this drill.
22	 *
23	 * It carries no stat strip, and that is a refusal rather than an omission.
24	 * Trend and Target are per section: sections differ in length and in the clock
25	 * they are run against, so "−6:52 across 3 runs" spanning two different
26	 * sections would be a number about nothing. Each section row carries its own
27	 * attempts instead, and the four numbers appear on the section's own page,
28	 * where they mean something.
29	 */
30	export function SectionLibraryLaunch({
31	  title,
32	  subtitle,
33	  instructions,
34	  children,
35	  onExit,
36	}: SectionLibraryLaunchProps) {
37	  return (
38	    <SectionLaunchFrame
39	      title={title}
40	      subtitle={subtitle}
41	      instructions={instructions}
42	      onExit={onExit}
43	      showBreadcrumbs={false}
44	      sectionsFirst
45	    >
46	      <div className="sl-runs">
47	        <div>
48	          <span className="sl-label">Your sections</span>
49	          <div style={{ marginTop: 12 }}>{children}</div>
50	        </div>
51	
52	        <aside className="sl-aside">
53	          <span className="sl-label">Before you start</span>
54	          <p>
55	            Read each piece silently. Then look away and say it back in your own
56	            words. Give your reaction out loud too. After the final piece,
57	            recall the whole argument out loud from memory. Your entire section
58	            is recorded in <strong>one take</strong>, and you cannot pause the
59	            recording.
60	          </p>
61	          <p>
62	            Every completed attempt stays here. If you run a section again, it
63	            is saved as a new attempt. Your earlier attempts are never replaced.
64	          </p>
65	        </aside>
66	      </div>
67	    </SectionLaunchFrame>
68	  );
69	}

242	/** Her last few runs, and what each one covered. */
243	function RunHistory({ state }: { state: HistoryState }) {
244	  if (state.kind === "idle") return null;
245	
246	  return (
247	    <div className="sl-runs">
248	      <div>
249	        <span className="sl-label">
250	          {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length > 0
251	            ? state.runs.length === 1
252	              ? "Your first run"
253	              : `Your last ${state.runs.length} runs`
254	            : "Your recent runs"}
255	        </span>
256	        {state.kind === "loading" && (
257	          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
258	            <span className="sl-skel" style={{ display: "block", width: "70%" }} />
259	          </p>
260	        )}
261	        {state.kind === "failed" && (
262	          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
263	            Your run history is not available right now. The section still starts
264	            normally.
265	          </p>
266	        )}
267	        {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length === 0 && (
268	          <p className="sl-note" style={{ marginTop: 10 }}>
269	            No runs yet. The first one is the baseline, not the test.
270	          </p>
271	        )}
272	        {state.kind === "ready" && state.runs.length > 0 && (
273	          <div className="sl-scroll">
274	            <table className="sl-rtable">
275	              <thead>
276	                <tr>
277	                  <th>Date</th>
278	                  <th>Time</th>
279	                  <th>Recalled</th>
280	                  <th className="sl-r">Stimulus by stimulus</th>
281	                </tr>
282	              </thead>
283	              <tbody>
284	                {state.runs.map((run) => {
285	                  const t = ticksFor(run);
286	                  // `created_at` rather than `graded_at`: a regrade rewrites the
287	                  // second one, and the date beside a stable sort has to be as
288	                  // stable as the sort.
289	                  const when = run.created_at ?? run.graded_at;
290	                  return (
291	                    <tr key={`${run.assignment_id}-${run.attempt_no}`}>
292	                      <td>
293	                        {new Date(when).toLocaleDateString(undefined, {
294	                          day: "numeric",
295	                          month: "short",
296	                        })}
297	                      </td>
298	                      <td>{clock(run.section.total_seconds)}</td>
299	                      <td>{t ? `${t.recorded} of ${t.slots}` : "not recorded"}</td>
300	                      <td className="sl-r">
301	                        {t ? (
302	                          <span
303	                            className="sl-ticks"
304	                            aria-label={`${t.recorded} of ${t.slots} stimuli recorded`}
305	                          >
306	                            {t.ticks.map((s, i) => (
307	                              <span key={i} className="sl-tick" data-state={s} />
308	                            ))}
309	                          </span>
310	                        ) : (
311	                          <span className="sl-note">not recorded</span>
312	                        )}
313	                      </td>
314	                    </tr>
315	                  );
316	                })}
317	              </tbody>
318	            </table>
319	          </div>
320	        )}
321	      </div>
322	
323	      <aside className="sl-aside">
324	        <span className="sl-label">Before you start</span>
325	        <p>
326	          Read each piece silently. Then look away and say it back in your own

1	import { useMemo } from "react";
2	import { motion, useReducedMotion, useTransform, type MotionValue } from "framer-motion";
3	
4	/**
5	 * The dusk meadow behind the Read, Recall, Colour door.
6	 *
7	 * Painted, not photographed, and painted at render time: every ridge is a
8	 * seeded sum of sines, every blade of grass a seeded quadratic, so the scene
9	 * ships as geometry rather than as three PNGs that have to arrive. It is one
10	 * picture cut into five depth planes -- sky, far hills, mid hills, near hills,
11	 * foreground grass -- and each plane is its own layer so the door can move them
12	 * at different rates as she scrolls. The demo card sits BETWEEN the near hills
13	 * and the grass, which is what makes the grass pass in front of it.
14	 *
15	 * Palette is the runner's own indigo field lifted into a sky, so the door and
16	 * the drill room read as one evening.
17	 */
18	
19	const W = 1600;
20	const H = 900;
21	
22	function mulberry32(seed: number) {
23	  let t = seed;
24	  return () => {
25	    t += 0x6d2b79f5;
26	    let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
27	    r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
28	    return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
29	  };
30	}
31	
32	/** A smooth ridge: a few sines summed, closed to the bottom edge. */
33	function ridge(seed: number, base: number, amp: number): string {
34	  const rng = mulberry32(seed);
35	  const waves = Array.from({ length: 4 }, (_, i) => ({
36	    f: (0.6 + rng() * 1.4) * (i + 1) * 0.0018,
37	    p: rng() * Math.PI * 2,
38	    a: amp / (i + 1.2),
39	  }));
40	  const pts: string[] = [];
41	  for (let x = -40; x <= W + 40; x += 16) {
42	    const y = waves.reduce((s, w) => s + w.a * Math.sin(w.f * x + w.p), base);
43	    pts.push(`${x.toFixed(1)} ${y.toFixed(1)}`);
44	  }
45	  // Closed 700 units below the frame, not at its edge: the plane rises as she
46	  // scrolls, and the skirt is what keeps the ground beneath it painted.
47	  return `M -40 ${H + 700} L ${pts.join(" L ")} L ${W + 40} ${H + 700} Z`;
48	}
49	
50	interface Blade {
51	  d: string;
52	  w: number;
53	  c: string;
54	  delay: number;
55	  x: number;
56	}
57	interface Flower {
58	  x: number;
59	  y: number;
60	  r: number;
61	  c: string;
62	}
63	
64	const GREENS = ["#4f6d55", "#5f7d64", "#6f8f74", "#7d9a80", "#8fa896"];
65	const FLOWERS = ["#f2b544", "#f7d27a", "#f6f3ea", "#c9c3ec", "#f3c9a0"];
66	
67	function grass(seed: number, count: number): { blades: Blade[]; flowers: Flower[] } {
68	  const rng = mulberry32(seed);
69	  const blades: Blade[] = [];
70	  const flowers: Flower[] = [];
71	  for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
72	    const x = -20 + rng() * (W + 40);
73	    const h = 36 + rng() * 96;
74	    const lean = (rng() - 0.5) * 60;
75	    const y0 = H + 10;
76	    blades.push({
77	      x,
78	      d: `M ${x.toFixed(1)} ${y0} Q ${(x + lean * 0.35).toFixed(1)} ${(y0 - h * 0.62).toFixed(1)} ${(x + lean).toFixed(1)} ${(y0 - h).toFixed(1)}`,
79	      w: 2.5 + rng() * 4,
80	      c: GREENS[Math.floor(rng() * GREENS.length)],
81	      delay: rng() * 4,
82	    });
83	    if (rng() < 0.22) {
84	      flowers.push({
85	        x: x + lean,
86	        y: y0 - h - 3,
87	        r: 3 + rng() * 4.5,
88	        c: FLOWERS[Math.floor(rng() * FLOWERS.length)],
89	      });
90	    }
91	  }
92	  return { blades, flowers };
93	}
94	
95	export interface MeadowSceneProps {
96	  /** 0 at the top of the hero, 1 when the hero has scrolled fully past. */
97	  progress: MotionValue<number>;
98	  /** Content drawn between the near hills and the foreground grass. */
99	  children?: React.ReactNode;
100	}
101	
102	const SVG_PROPS = {
103	  viewBox: `0 0 ${W} ${H}`,
104	  preserveAspectRatio: "xMidYMax slice",
105	  "aria-hidden": true,
106	  focusable: false,
107	} as const;
108	
109	export function MeadowScene({ progress, children }: MeadowSceneProps) {
110	  const reduced = useReducedMotion();
111	
112	  const far = useMemo(() => ridge(11, 545, 42), []);
113	  const mid = useMemo(() => ridge(23, 632, 58), []);
114	  const near = useMemo(() => ridge(37, 728, 46), []);
115	  const ground = useMemo(() => ridge(51, 862, 12), []);
116	  const { blades, flowers } = useMemo(() => grass(71, 170), []);
117	
118	  // Each plane rides the scroll at its own rate. Negative is "up and away":
119	  // the foreground rises fastest so it crosses in front of whatever sits
120	  // behind it, which is the entire trick.
121	  const skyY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, 60]);
122	  const farY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -40]);
123	  const midY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -110]);
124	  const nearY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -190]);
125	  const stageY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -230]);
126	  const grassY = useTransform(progress, [0, 1], [0, -420]);
127	
128	  const still = reduced ? { y: 0 } : undefined;
129	
130	  return (
131	    <div className="mw" aria-hidden={children ? undefined : true}>
132	      <motion.svg {...SVG_PROPS} className="mw-layer mw-sky" style={still ?? { y: skyY }}>
133	        <defs>
134	          <linearGradient id="mw-sky-g" x1="0" y1="0" x2="0" y2="1">
135	            <stop offset="0" stopColor="#17132e" />
136	            <stop offset="0.34" stopColor="#352c6a" />
137	            <stop offset="0.56" stopColor="#675ba0" />
138	            <stop offset="0.66" stopColor="#a89fe0" />
139	            <stop offset="0.73" stopColor="#dcc5d6" />
140	            <stop offset="0.79" stopColor="#f6d6b3" />
141	            <stop offset="1" stopColor="#f3c9a0" />
142	          </linearGradient>
143	          <radialGradient id="mw-sun" cx="0.5" cy="0.66" r="0.45">
144	            <stop offset="0" stopColor="#fff2d9" stopOpacity="0.95" />
145	            <stop offset="0.35" stopColor="#f8d9b0" stopOpacity="0.55" />
146	            <stop offset="1" stopColor="#f3c9a0" stopOpacity="0" />
147	          </radialGradient>
148	        </defs>
149	        <rect width={W} height={H} fill="url(#mw-sky-g)" />
150	        <rect width={W} height={H} fill="url(#mw-sun)" />
151	      </motion.svg>
152	
153	      <motion.svg {...SVG_PROPS} className="mw-layer mw-far" style={still ?? { y: farY }}>
154	        <defs>
155	          <linearGradient id="mw-far-g" x1="0" y1="0" x2="0" y2="1">
156	            <stop offset="0.55" stopColor="#b9b0dd" />
157	            <stop offset="1" stopColor="#d8cfe6" />
158	          </linearGradient>
159	          <filter id="mw-soft" x="-5%" y="-5%" width="110%" height="110%">
160	            <feGaussianBlur stdDeviation="5" />
161	          </filter>
162	        </defs>
163	        <path d={far} fill="url(#mw-far-g)" filter="url(#mw-soft)" />
164	      </motion.svg>
165	
166	      <motion.svg {...SVG_PROPS} className="mw-layer mw-mid" style={still ?? { y: midY }}>
167	        <defs>
168	          <linearGradient id="mw-mid-g" x1="0" y1="0" x2="0" y2="1">
169	            <stop offset="0.65" stopColor="#9fb1a8" />
170	            <stop offset="1" stopColor="#c3cfbe" />
171	          </linearGradient>
172	          <filter id="mw-soft2" x="-5%" y="-5%" width="110%" height="110%">
173	            <feGaussianBlur stdDeviation="2.2" />
174	          </filter>
175	        </defs>
176	        <path d={mid} fill="url(#mw-mid-g)" filter="url(#mw-soft2)" />
177	      </motion.svg>
178	
179	      <motion.svg {...SVG_PROPS} className="mw-layer mw-near" style={still ?? { y: nearY }}>
180	        <defs>
181	          <linearGradient id="mw-near-g" x1="0" y1="0" x2="0" y2="1">
182	            <stop offset="0.75" stopColor="#8aa68f" />
183	            <stop offset="1" stopColor="#a9bfa4" />
184	          </linearGradient>
185	        </defs>
186	        <path d={near} fill="url(#mw-near-g)" />
187	      </motion.svg>
188	
189	      {children && (
190	        <motion.div className="mw-stage" style={still ?? { y: stageY }}>
191	          {children}
192	        </motion.div>
193	      )}
194	
195	      <motion.svg {...SVG_PROPS} className="mw-layer mw-grass" style={still ?? { y: grassY }}>
196	        <defs>
197	          <linearGradient id="mw-ground-g" x1="0" y1="0" x2="0" y2="1">
198	            <stop offset="0" stopColor="#5f7d64" />
199	            <stop offset="1" stopColor="#3f5a47" />
200	          </linearGradient>
201	        </defs>
202	        <path d={ground} fill="url(#mw-ground-g)" />
203	        <g className="mw-blades" fill="none" strokeLinecap="round">
204	          {blades.map((b, i) => (
205	            <path
206	              key={i}
207	              d={b.d}
